| 2026-08-25 | Manus | **Production performance correction — Individual ERT dashboard and My Shift loading:** The slow provider experience was traced to duplicate activation polling on Today, a seven-day ERT query on a home surface that only needs the current team, repeated legacy UTL read-repair for teams that already had a live ERTL projection, eager loading of the heavier institutional duty card on My Shift entry, and unbounded historical duty-summary reads. Protected PR [#584](https://github.com/karuejob-max/paeds_resus_app/pull/584) reduces Today to a current-team horizon, reuses the global non-miss activation poll without starting a second timer, defers non-urgent duty/readiness queries until after first paint, and keeps an explicit loading state so the UI never falsely claims that no action is waiting. My Shift now loads a short current window first, keeps future teams behind deliberate expansion, defers institutional responsibilities until opened, batches exact-shift read-repair, skips teams with a live ERTL projection, filters ended/expired/superseded role rows, and caps live activation response payloads at the 20 most recent cases. The provider duty summary is bounded to the previous day through 90 days ahead; history remains available through institutional history views. Exact institution/pole/membership/assignment/acceptance and activation authorization rules are unchanged. **Validation:** focused IERS/provider/Today tests passed (30 tests across 8 files); clinical lint, `git diff --check`, and production build passed (`built in 13.02s`); the full local TypeScript process was terminated by the sandbox resource limit, while protected CI passed; PR #584 squash-merged to `origin/main` as `5c0e67c1`. Render propagated the release: public root returned HTTP 200 and live `ProviderToday-qn3YTpcF.js` and `ProviderMyShift-DG589Lh_.js` assets contained `horizonDays:0`, deferred `Institution responsibilities`, `Open when needed`, and `View more future teams` markers. No production activation, responder, duty, readiness, report, drill, patient, schema, or migration data was created or changed. **Remaining:** authenticated phone timing check using `paedsresus254@gmail.com` is recommended.

| 2026-08-25 | Manus | **Production defect resolved — activation creation was reported as failed after a successful insert:** Read-only production diagnosis confirmed that the provider-facing `Activation could not be created` message occurred immediately after the `iersActivationEvents` insert, before team snapshot, resource seeding, responder creation, or notification fan-out. The root cause was unreliable Drizzle/mysql2 exposure of `insertId` on this insert path; the event could exist while the extracted ID was empty, causing a false failure. Protected PR [#582](https://github.com/karuejob-max/paeds_resus_app/pull/582) now generates an opaque server-side nonce before inserting the activation event, re-fetches the event ID by that nonce, and uses the same safe correlation pattern for downtime reconciliation. No schema migration was needed. **Validation:** focused IERS, activation, QR, current-shift, provider-card, and Today tests passed (30 tests across 8 files); clinical lint, `git diff --check`, and production build passed (`built in 13.98s`); protected CI passed; the PR squash-merged to `origin/main` as `33bb0632`; Render then served the current entry bundle and public root returned HTTP 200. No production activation, responder, assignment, readiness, drill, report, patient, or test record was created or modified. **Remaining:** authenticated phone verification using `paedsresus254@gmail.com` must use a real emergency workflow only; do not create a test activation. |

## How to use this file

- **Before you start work:** Read `docs/PAEDS_RESUS_COHERENT_PICTURE.md` (mandatory entry point), then `PLATFORM_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` and this file (full).
- **When you complete something:** Add a dated entry under Done (who, what, commit/PR if any).
- **When you start something:** Add or update In progress (who, what).
- **When something is blocked or needs a decision:** Add to Blocked / needs decision.
- **When you review another's work:** Add to Critique / review (date, who you are commenting on, what you checked, any issues or suggestions). The next agent or Job can then act on it.

| 2026-08-21 | Manus | **Institution-name onboarding fix:** Post-deployment reproduction showed a Zod `too_small` error because the final request could carry an empty or short `institutionName`. Root cause was split state: `FacilityAutocomplete` maintained its own `query`, while the parent onboarding form only updated `formData.institutionName` after a registry selection or explicit manual-entry action. Manual typing could therefore leave the parent value empty at final submit. Fixed by synchronizing every facility-name keystroke to the parent, adding an early Step 1 validation message, trimming the final payload, and trimming server-side before the existing minimum-length check. Added a focused component regression test. **Verification:** `pnpm run check` passed; `pnpm run build` passed; focused component + institutional router tests passed; CI gate passed on PR [#455](https://github.com/karuejob-max/paeds_resus_app/pull/455). Squashed merge commit: `aed2b9e`. **Prod:** no local `.env` values are required for this code change; deploy from `main`, then run one real onboarding smoke test using a facility name of at least 3 characters. | PR [#455](https://github.com/karuejob-max/paeds_resus_app/pull/455) |

| 2026-08-21 | Manus | **Institutional onboarding final-submit fix:** New institutional users could reach the last step but receive a validation error when the optional Registration Number field was left blank. Root cause was a contract mismatch: the UI submits an empty string (`""`), while `institution.completeOnboarding` used `z.string().min(1).optional()`; an empty string is present and therefore fails `min(1)`. Fixed defensively in both directions: the client sends blank registration numbers as `undefined`, and the server preprocesses whitespace-only values to `undefined` before applying the optional non-empty validation. Added a focused regression test covering the final mutation with a blank registration number. **Verification:** `pnpm run check` passed; `pnpm run build` passed; focused regression test passed; `git diff --check` passed; CI gate passed on PR [#453](https://github.com/karuejob-max/paeds_resus_app/pull/453). Squashed merge commit: `e0f0307`. **Prod:** code is merged; confirm/apply `pnpm run db:apply-0071` in the production environment if the institutional multi-admin tables and `registrationNumber` column are not already present, then repeat one real onboarding smoke test. | PR [#453](https://github.com/karuejob-max/paeds_resus_app/pull/453) |

| 2026-08-12 | Claude | **Fixed a real, live grading bug the CEO found while reviewing courses: "Instructor" course Module 4 ("Skills testing, objective evaluation & debriefing") marked the objectively correct answer on question 2 wrong, blocking progression.** Root cause traced to actual code, not guessed: `ensure-instructor-course-catalog.ts` and `ensure-bls-acls-catalog.ts` both stored `quizQuestions.correctAnswer` with `typeof q.correctAnswer === "string" ? q.correctAnswer : JSON.stringify(q.correctAnswer)` — since `correctAnswer` is *always* a string by its own type, that ternary's true branch always fires, meaning `correctAnswer` was stored **raw, never JSON-encoded**, contradicting the documented contract in `shared/quiz-answer-contract.ts` (`parseStoredQuizCorrectAnswer` expects `JSON.parse(correctAnswer)` to recover the option text). Most questions still graded correctly anyway, by accident — `parseStoredQuizCorrectAnswer` falls back to the raw trimmed string when `JSON.parse` throws, and a raw sentence normally *isn't* valid JSON. Module 4 Q2 was different: its correct answer is itself a quoted line of example dialogue (`"I noticed the epinephrine was delayed..."`), so the raw stored string, quote marks and all, *is* itself valid JSON — `JSON.parse` silently stripped those quote marks on read, while the option text the learner actually clicks still has them. The two could never match. **Confirmed with an actual reproduction, not just code-reading:** ran the exact old encode → parse → compare sequence in isolation and reproduced the reported "marked wrong" outcome directly, then confirmed the fix flips it to "marked right" and that a normal, non-quoted answer is unaffected (no regression). **Fix:** both seeders now call `JSON.stringify(q.correctAnswer)` unconditionally, matching what `ensure-heartsaver-aha-catalog.ts`/`ensure-heartsaver-catalog.ts` already did correctly — confirmed via grep that Heartsaver's seeders never had this bug, and that no other seeder in the codebase used the broken ternary pattern. Considered switching to the stricter `encodeQuizCorrectAnswerForStorage` helper (which validates the answer is actually one of the options and throws if not) but backed off deliberately: that validation could throw and take down catalog sync entirely for BLS or ACLS if any one of ~20+ questions had so much as a byte-level text mismatch I hadn't verified by hand, and the working, corrected `JSON.stringify` alone is enough to fix exactly what's broken without adding a new failure mode to something with much larger blast radius. **No separate data-repair migration needed** — the sync logic for both affected courses does `DELETE quizQuestions WHERE quizId=X` then re-inserts fresh on every access (confirmed by reading the code), and `userProgress` tracks quiz-level completion by `quizId`, not individual question row IDs, so the fix self-heals automatically the next time any learner accesses either course; nobody's prior progress needs resetting, unlike the BLS module removal. Learners who were incorrectly failed on this question will need to retry it once — no notification needed since they were never falsely marked complete, just correctly-if-confusingly blocked. **New regression test** added to `shared/quiz-answer-contract.test.ts`, using the real reported answer text as the fixture, verifying the buggy encoding fails to round-trip and the fixed encoding does. **Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, `pnpm run build` succeeds, unit gate **654/654** (was 653 — the new regression test), full `server/` suite (real database) **1917/1930** — same 12 pre-existing failures as every check this session, zero new. Re-fetched `origin/main` immediately before packaging — nothing new landed. **Prod: code-only, no migration, no schema change — self-heals on next course access, no learner notification needed.** | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

| 2026-08-11 | Claude | **Three CEO-requested UI/branding changes, unrelated to each other, done in one pass.** **1) "My Certificates" grouped into folders** (Life Support / Fellowship / CPD). Investigated the actual data first: `certificates.programType` enum only ever produces Life Support or Fellowship/Instructor rows — CPD certificates live in a completely separate system (`cpd.myCertificates`, `cpdAttendees` email-matched, `/api/cpd/certificate/:attendeeId` download route) that was never surfaced on this page before. Added that query alongside the existing one so the CPD folder is real, not empty. New `CertificateFolder.tsx` (small, presentational, collapsible) — grouping/filtering logic itself stays in `ProviderDashboard.tsx` since the folder component doesn't need to know about certificate types. Anything with a `programType` not in the Life Support list falls into Fellowship by default (documented in the code) rather than silently vanishing if a new type is ever added to the schema without updating this list. **2) "AHA Hub" renamed to "Life Support Hub."** Checked every file referencing `AhaHub`/`aha-hub` first (route paths, hook names, component names, test files) — the literal user-facing text "AHA Hub" only existed in `AHAPracticeLab.tsx` (3 places, breadcrumb/back-button labels), so only those were touched. Left every internal identifier, file name, and route path (`/aha-courses`) unchanged — renaming those would be a much bigger, riskier refactor for zero user-facing benefit. Also updated the nav label itself (`Header.tsx`, "AHA" → "Life Support") since it's the literal entry point into what just got renamed — a judgment call, not explicitly requested, flagged here rather than done silently. **3) Certificate-ready screen now names the actual course**, not "AHA." Two separate completion screens both said generic things ("AHA Cognitive Certificate Ready" / "Issue AHA Certificate" in one; "Download My Certificate" in the other) — neither is the exact literal phrase the CEO quoted, but both are functionally the moment described. Both now read "Paeds Resus {course title} Certificate" — e.g. "Paeds Resus BLS Certificate", "Paeds Resus Instructor Certificate" — using `course.title`/`dbCourse.title`, which was already available in both components' scope. Deliberately dropped the `isAhaCourse` conditional for this text entirely (it originally special-cased AHA vs. "Fellowship" wording) since the CEO's own example, "Paeds Resus Instructor Certificate," isn't an AHA course — the request is clearly meant to apply to any course completion, not just AHA ones. `isAhaCourse` itself is still used elsewhere in the same function (the coursework-duration label) — not removed, just no longer branching the certificate text. **Not run/verified:** no Node/DB toolchain in this sandbox session — brace/paren balance checks plus full `diff` review against `origin/main` on all five touched files, confirming every diff is exactly and only the intended change — **CEO should run `pnpm run check` before merging.** **Prod: no migration, no backend change, pure frontend.** | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

| 2026-08-11 | Claude | **Real production run of the BLS progress-reset migration succeeded on the data side, but a real sequencing mistake in my own instructions broke the notification step — caught from the CEO's pasted output, not self-reported, and fixed with a small standalone script rather than re-running the reset.** The instructions I gave told the CEO to run the reset migration (Step 1) *before* applying the code change containing the new `blsCourseContentUpdated` email template (Step 2) — backwards, since the migration's own notification step needs that template to exist. Real production output confirms the data side worked exactly as verified locally: course "BLS Provider Course — AHA 2025 Guidelines" (id 32), **25 progress rows reset across 5 learners**, the safety guard correctly skipped several empty "Test Course" rows (ids 33-39) and a second, separate, empty BLS course row (id 70, "Basic Life Support (BLS)" — from the secondary `ensure-bls-aha-catalog.ts` seed path noted 2026-08-10, present in prod but with zero enrollee progress, harmless). All 5 notification sends failed with `Template 'blsCourseContentUpdated' not found` — exactly the symptom of the template not existing yet, confirming the sequencing error rather than some other bug. **Did not re-run the reset** (would be redundant at best given confirmed idempotency, and touching already-correctly-reset data unnecessarily is exactly the kind of "fix" that risks making things worse). **New `scripts/resend-bls-content-update-notifications.mjs`:** a small, standalone, non-numbered script (no schema/data mutation beyond sending email, so no migration number needed) that looks up the exact 5 known-affected user IDs from the real run's output and sends just the notification, meant to run once the Step 2 code (with the template) is actually applied. **Verified the fix addresses the real cause, not just re-tried blindly:** seeded a local test database with users at those same 5 real IDs, ran the resend script against it — the failure mode changed from `Template 'blsCourseContentUpdated' not found` to `SENDGRID_API_KEY not configured` (expected and correct in this sandbox, which has no real email credentials), confirming the template lookup itself now succeeds once the code exists; the actual send will work in the CEO's environment with real SendGrid credentials already configured for the other live templates. **Verification:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. Re-fetched `origin/main` — nothing new landed. **Prod: corrected instructions given in chat — apply the Step 2 code first this time, then run the resend script once, not the original reset script again.** | _(resend script packaged as zip; see chat for corrected sequencing)_ |

| 2026-08-11 | Claude | **BLS course, Phase 1 complete: CEO decided resetting affected learners' progress (with notification) is acceptable, so the First Aid module removal is now ready to ship — migration number pending, everything else done and verified.** Builds on the 2026-08-10 entry above (which held this pending exactly this decision). **New `server/email-service.ts` template `blsCourseContentUpdated`:** explains the course was restructured to match AHA's real BLS curriculum, that First Aid content now lives in the separate Heartsaver course, and that the learner's progress on the affected modules was reset — plain, honest framing, not buried in jargon. **New `scripts/apply-XXXX-bls-first-aid-progress-reset.mjs`** (number pending reservation — CEO to run `reserve-migration-number.ps1` and substitute it themselves per the chat instructions, to avoid a round-trip): finds the live BLS course's modules at order >= 5 (the ones about to be repurposed/removed), resets any learner progress on them, and emails each affected learner once. Written to run via `tsx` (imports `sendEmail`/`getDb` directly from `server/`), not plain `node` like most `apply-*` scripts — same pattern already established for `verify:raw-narrative-immutable`. **Two safety properties built in and actually verified against real data, not just reasoned about:** (1) refuses to run if the BLS course already has fewer than 7 modules — the signal that the code deploy already happened and running this now would act on already-repurposed content instead of the original First Aid modules it needs to identify against; (2) idempotent — a second run finds nothing left to reset. **Verified end-to-end, not just unit-level:** seeded a fake pre-existing 9-module BLS course into a real test database, with a learner mid-course holding progress on both an affected module (order 7) and an unaffected one (order 2), then ran the actual script against it. Confirmed by direct SQL query afterward: exactly the 3 affected progress rows were deleted, the unaffected one survived untouched. Ran it a second time — correctly found nothing left to do. Manually deleted the extra modules to simulate the code already being deployed — the safety guard correctly refused to act. **Sequencing, stated plainly because getting it backwards would defeat the whole point:** this script must run against the *old* 9-module BLS course, before the code change (already written, see 2026-08-10 entry: `bls-modules-data.ts` trimmed to 6 modules) is deployed — not after. **Verification of the code itself:** `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, `pnpm run build` succeeds, unit gate **653/653**, full `server/` suite (real database) **1917/1930** — same 12 pre-existing failures as every check this session, zero new. Re-fetched `origin/main` immediately before packaging — nothing new landed. **Prod: this is a live-data migration with a required ordering constraint — see the chat instructions for the exact apply-then-deploy sequence; do not merge the code PR before the migration script has been run and its output confirmed.** | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat; migration number to be filled in by CEO)_ |

| 2026-08-06 | Claude | **Process fix for the PR #407 branch-pollution incident, requested by the CEO: a written rule plus an automated backstop, not just one or the other.** Root cause of #407 (see the 2026-08-06 entry two above): at least two different sessions started new, unrelated work by building on top of whatever branch happened to be checked out locally, instead of branching fresh from `main` — a CI-only PR ended up with an entire unrelated AHA course-type feature and a separate Code Signal system, each merged in as its own commit onto the CI branch. **AGENTS.md:** tightened the "Ship code" rule to explicitly require `git checkout main && git fetch origin main && git merge origin/main --ff-only` before starting any new task, with the #407 incident cited inline as the concrete cost of skipping it — not left implicit the way the old wording ("feature branch → `gh pr create`...") was, which never actually said branch *from what*. **New `scripts/verify-branch-base.mjs`:** a backstop for when the written rule gets missed anyway (which is exactly what happened, twice, even with the CEO's existing collision-protocol docs already in place) — checks the current branch's actual fork point against `origin/main` and warns (does not block) on: (1) merge commits pulling in other named branches, the single clearest signature of what happened on #407; (2) a stale fork point, many commits behind main's current tip; (3) an unusually wide diff for what's presumably one task. Wired into `scripts/pre-merge-check.ps1` (runs automatically, first, before the existing four-file collision check) and also exposed standalone as `pnpm run check:branch-base`. **Didn't just write the check and assume it works — tested it against the real incident:** ran it against the actual `ci/mysql-service` branch (still fetchable from origin) and confirmed it correctly flags both the two suspicious merge commits by name and the 41-file width, with the exact same wording used in the AGENTS.md incident note. Also confirmed it correctly says nothing on `main` itself and on a genuinely fresh test branch. **Caught this same failure pattern recurring live, while building the fix for it:** the `origin/main` fetch immediately before writing this entry showed a brand new merge, `Merge feat/instructor-landing-route into feat/instructor-course-complete`, landed in the interim — i.e. exactly the pattern this tooling targets happened again during this session, on a different branch, before the fix could even ship. Didn't touch it — fixing it means understanding what it was actually meant to display, which needs its own look, not a guess bundled into this PR. **New `ProgressAndLedgerCard` (`LearnerDashboard.tsx`):** overall % across Phase 1/2/3 plus a per-phase detail breakdown (Phase 2 shows the same leader/member counts as `Phase2BookingCard`, not duplicated logic, just re-read), and the payment ledger UI — paid/remaining plus an M-Pesa STK-push form. **A real usability bug caught and fixed before shipping, not after:** `initiateSTKPush` requires the phone number in exact `254XXXXXXXXX` format via a hard regex; the obvious naive UI (plain text input, no normalization) would have thrown "Invalid phone number" for the majority of learners typing their own number the way they normally would (`07...`, `+254...`). Added client-side normalization for the common formats before the call, not just a format hint. **Scoping decision, not a spec ambiguity:** STK push amount defaults to KES 2,500 (or the remaining balance if smaller) rather than a free-form amount field — matches the existing monthly-instalment pattern, and the spec only asked for phone-number-in/STK-push-out, not an amount picker. **New pre-existing baseline discrepancy found while verifying, not caused by this PR:** full `server/` suite now shows 8 failures (was 7) — the new one is `server/cpd/cpd.test.ts`, a `db.select(...).where is not a function` error inside `cpd.ts` from the CPD intelligence engine (PR #382/#385), confirmed pre-existing by reproducing it with this session's changes stashed. `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, `pnpm run check` full pipeline green, unit gate **635/635** (both previously-skipped tests now pass — from upstream changes, not this PR), full `server/` suite **1870/1922** (8 pre-existing failures, one new since the last entry but confirmed unrelated, see above). **This closes out INST-25** — all four slices of the IERP/NERP v2 respec are now built: Phase 1 elearning gate, Phase 2 role-based booking (backend + frontend), and this payment/progress/ledger slice. **Prod: code-only, no migration.** | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

That triggered a full re-spec, not just a bug fix. **New canonical doc: `docs/IERP_NERP_PROGRAM_V2_SPEC.md`** — the complete design, recorded in full so it isn't lost between sessions: (1) naming — "Nurse Cohort Program" → "Nurses Emergency Readiness Program (NERP)" for parity with IERP; flagged, not resolved, that IERP-as-intern-track and the future "Institutional Emergency Readiness Program" umbrella collide on the same acronym at different scopes. Wired into both mutations; the update path only re-checks when something that could actually change the overlap window changed (instructor, date, or either time), to avoid a wasted query on unrelated edits. **2) Notifications for the two silent-promotion gaps flagged in the last two entries:** new `bookingWaitlistPromoted` and `mentorshipTierPromoted` email templates (reusing the existing `sendEmail` template infra, no new notification system), fired from `cancelHandsOnSession`'s waitlist-promotion branch and both promotion branches in `confirmMentorshipGroup` (provisional→qualified, qualified→lead_instructor). | 2026-07-29 | Claude | **Care Signal redaction job — real per-row retry tracking and exponential backoff, migration 0081 (renumbered twice: 0079 collided with the same-window fellowship-grandfathering PR #362, moved to 0080; then 0080 itself collided with PR #363's CPD grace-tracking column, caught by the CEO directly rather than by re-checking before packaging — three sessions claiming migration numbers in close succession is now a recurring pattern worth naming plainly, not just individually patching each time it happens).** Closes the second gap from the 2026-07-29 redaction code review (the first, the LLM kill switch, shipped separately in PR #360): before this, the only signal was `redactedNarrative IS NULL` = pending, with no way to distinguish "not yet attempted" from "has failed 40 times" — a permanently-unredactable narrative (LLM safety-filter refusal, malformed input, anything non-transient) would retry every single 10-minute cron tick forever, with zero operator visibility, and the existing "back-off" was really just "stop this batch early on a 429" with no actual delay before the next tick. **Schema:** three new columns on `careSignalEvents` — `redaction_attempts` (int, default 0), `redaction_last_attempt_at` (nullable timestamp), `redaction_last_error` (nullable text, truncated to 2000 chars). **Logic:** `isEligibleForRetry` computes real exponential backoff — 10min, 20min, 40min... doubling per attempt, capped at 24h — and `MAX_REDACTION_ATTEMPTS` (8) stops automatic retries entirely once exhausted (attempt 8 backs off ~21h, still under the 24h cap in practice — the cap is a safety net for a hypothetically higher limit, not something attempt 8 itself reaches; an earlier doc-comment draft claimed otherwise and was corrected before shipping). The SQL query excludes exhausted rows outright (`redactionAttempts < MAX_REDACTION_ATTEMPTS`); backoff-window filtering happens in application code against a slightly oversized candidate pool, since per-row exponential comparisons don't read cleanly as a Drizzle WHERE clause. Both `redactionAttempts`/`redactionLastAttemptAt` update on every attempt, success or failure; `redactionLastError` only on failure, cleared on success. **A real bug caught by careful review before shipping, not after:** the two existing DB-dependent tests mocked candidate rows without the new fields at all — `isEligibleForRetry(undefined, undefined, now)` would have thrown on `undefined.getTime()`. Fixed two ways: made the function itself defensive (missing/undefined data fails open — eligible now — rather than crashing), and corrected the two tests' mock fixtures to include the new fields properly, since a defensively-coded function shouldn't excuse an inaccurate test fixture. 7 new pure-function tests (`care-signal-redact-backoff.test.ts`) covering the never-attempted case, the exact backoff-window boundary (one minute before vs. exactly at), doubling across three consecutive attempts, and the cap behavior in isolation from `MAX_REDACTION_ATTEMPTS` (which the pure function doesn't itself enforce — that's the caller's SQL filter). `tsc --noEmit` clean, all four related test files passing (12 passing, 2 correctly skipped for lacking `DATABASE_URL`), full `vitest.unit.config.ts` gate re-verified fresh against current `main` (post PR #360, #361, #362, #363) immediately before packaging the final time per step 1b. **Prod: migration `db:apply-0081` required post-deploy** — adds three nullable/defaulted columns only; existing pending rows default to `redaction_attempts=0`, immediately eligible for retry under the new logic, nothing gets stuck by the migration itself. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

rging the two systems.** CEO cross-checked the newly-merged general cadre taxonomy (`client/src/lib/cadre-taxonomy.ts`, `users.cadre` — deep, multi-level, platform-wide, feeding CPD/CNE) against this program's own `institutionalStaffMembers.designation` (3 business-rule buckets: intern/nurse/other) and asked for my honest assessment on whether to adopt the deeper taxonomy directly. **Recommendation, and what got built:** don't replace `designation` with taxonomy matching — the taxonomy is free-text (`varchar(128)`, client-validated only), not a database-enforced enum, and the cohort program's actual business rules (subsidised pricing, which payment gate applies) only need 3 buckets regardless of how many nursing sub-cadres exist. Matching against 20+ free-text leaves to decide eligibility would add real risk (an unmapped new sub-cadre silently falling through) for no corresponding business gain. **Instead:** `shared/cadre-designation-mapping.ts`'s `inferDesignationFromCadre(cadre)` auto-applies designation only for the genuinely unambiguous cases — any RN-family cadre leaf (any level, any sub-specialty, per CEO: "not bothering which subspecialty") maps to `permanent_nurse`; `NOI`/`MOI` map 1:1. **`COI` deliberately excluded from auto-mapping** — the taxonomy has one flat `COI` value with no BSc/Diploma split, so guessing between `coi_bsc`/`coi_diploma` would be a silent, possibly-wrong eligibility decision; flagged rather than guessed. Wired at two points: `syncProviderProfileFacility` (`server/services/facility-registry.service.ts`) applies the inference the moment a pending institutional link is created (previously always defaulted to `"other"`); `DesignationDeclarationCard` (`LearnerDashboard.tsx`) pre-selects the same inference as a fallback for staff records that predate the cadre being set, shown as an editable pre-fill with a visible note, not a lock — in case it's wrong. **Known drift risk, flagged not hidden:** the RN-family leaf list (13 values) is hand-maintained in the new file rather than imported from `cadre-taxonomy.ts`, since that's a client-only path and importing it into `shared`/`server` code is fragile across build targets — cross-checked by hand against the real taxonomy file this session (all 13 values confirmed present verbatim, including through a same-day rename of the taxonomy's export from `CNE_CADRE_TAXONOMY` to `CPD_CADRE_TAXONOMY` that didn't touch any values). 9 new tests across `shared/cadre-designation-mapping.test.ts` (every RN leaf, NOI/MOI, COI-not-guessed, doctor/student/null all return null). `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, unit gate **637/639** (2 pre-existing skips, unrelated), full `server/` suite **1851/1904** (same 7 pre-existing `e2e-tests.test.ts` failures, unchanged). Re-synced against fresh `origin/main` mid-session after a same-day CNE->CPD rename landed — confirmed it only renamed the taxonomy's export, no value changes, before continuing. **Prod: code-only, no migration.** | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

ResusGPS diagnosis/investigation assistance, content generation, personalization, etc.) will now throw a correctly-typed `LlmFeaturesDisabledError` but have not been individually audited for graceful "paused" line every 10 minutes instead of silently retrying every pending narrative. **Caught and fixed a real test interaction while building this:** the existing DB-dependent redaction tests would | 2026-07-21 | Claude | **Instructor pathway: per-course competency + mentorship tiers (Provisional -> Qualified -> Lead Instructor), plus Instructor Course content expanded from a 1-module MVP stub to 6 real modules.** CEO decisions, built together over this session: (1) **per-course competency** — an instructor's global approval/certification flags no longer imply they're qualified to teach every course; they must have personally completed a given provider course (BLS/ACLS/PALS/etc.) themselves. New `instructorQualifications` table (userId, programType), auto-populated by `syncInstructorQualificationsForUser` (`server/lib/instructor-qualifications.ts`) whenever either "just became instructor-certified" or "just completed a provider course themselves" becomes newly true — whichever direction completes second. Enforced server-side in `signOffPracticalSkills` (not just a dropdown filter — the sign-off itself is blocked if the instructor isn't qualified for that specific course) and used to filter `institution.listAssignableInstructors` by `programType`, wired into both the create and edit schedule forms in `HospitalAdminDashboard.tsx` (they needed separate queries — course-specific qualification means the create form's selected course and the edit form's selected course can differ, so they can't share one instructor list). (2) **Mentorship tiers**, CEO-specified: **Provisional** (completed Instructor Course, admin-approved, paired with one named mentor for their whole provisional period — `instructorMentorships`, `menteeUserId` unique) -> **Qualified** (mentor manually confirms 3 independently-led groups, start to finish, across all three phases — `instructorMentorshipGroups`, auto-promotes at 3) -> **Lead Instructor** (mentored 10 different mentees to Qualified — auto-promotes at 10). **Debeliberately manual confirmation, not auto-computed from attendance data** — CEO's own reasoning, matched in the build: whether a group was genuinely led well and independently is a real credentialing judgment call. New mutations in `instructor.ts`: `assignMentor` (admin-only), `confirmMentorshipGroup`, `setInstructorTierOverride` (admin-only bootstrap for instructors the CEO trained directly before this system existed — solves the cold-start problem where nobody would otherwise ever qualify as a mentor), `getMyMentees`. **Naming correction mid-build:** the top tier was originally going to be called "Faculty" — caught a real conflict before shipping: a same-day `North Star v2.1` addendum (adopted separately, docs-only) defines an unrelated "Fellow Instructor" tier for the Fellowship program, and explicitly rejects a "train the trainer of trainers" model for that system specifically. CEO confirmed the two systems are genuinely separate (Cohort Program vs. Fellowship, different product lines) and the reasoning doesn't conflict, but asked for the name changed anyway since "Faculty" was close enough to "Fellow"/"Fellowship" to mislead a future reader — renamed to **Lead Instructor** throughout (schema enum value `lead_instructor`, migration, router, course content). **Instructor Course content**: expanded from one module with a single quiz question to 6 modules — adult learning principles & facilitation (Tell-Show-Do-Practice-Feedback, psychological safety), running Phase 1-3 to a consistent standard, objective skills testing & GAS-structured debriefing (advocacy-inquiry feedback), the mentorship pathway itself, and platform tools/QA — matching the structure of international (AHA-style) instructor courses, per request. Idempotency is per-module (matched by title), not "does any module exist for this course" — the original MVP check would have silently prevented modules 2-6 from ever seeding on an already-migrated environment. **Migration 0075** (`scripts/apply-0075-instructor-competency-mentorship.mjs`, `pnpm run db:apply-0075`): adds `users.instructorTier` and the three new tables, same idempotent columnExists/tableExists pattern as prior migrations. **Known gaps, not addressed here:** no frontend UI yet for `assignMentor`/`confirmMentorshipGroup`/`getMyMentees` — API-only for now; and the three gaps from the earlier instructor-pathway audit (double-booking protection, batch sign-off, assignment notifications) remain open, out of scope for this pass. `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, unit gate **608/608**, full `server/` suite **1831/1882** (same 7 pre-existing `e2e-tests.test.ts` failures, unchanged). | 2026-07-20 | Claude | **Fellowship recovery-code O(n) scaling limit — closed, migration 0073 (renumbered from 0072 at the CEO's request to avoid a collision with a parallel session already using 0072 — see AGENTS.md's collision protocol; checked fresh against `origin/main` first, `db:apply-0072` wasn't registered there yet, so this was a real-time heads-up from the CEO rather than something git itself could have caught).** The other smaller documented debt from the 2026-07-19 platform audit, and the one that genuinely needed a schema change: recovery-code lookup was bcrypt-hashed and therefore one-way, so `findFellowshipTokenByRecoveryCode` fetched every token row and bcrypt-compared each one — fine at pilot scale, explicitly flagged as needing an index before a few thousand tokens. **Fix:** new nullable `fellowshipTokens.recoveryCodeLookupHash` (VARCHAR(64), indexed) — a deterministic SHA-256 HMAC of the normalized code, keyed by the existing `JWT_SECRET` with a domain-separation label (`server/lib/fellowship-token.ts`'s new `hashRecoveryCodeForLookup`) rather than introducing a brand-new required production secret. **This is never the actual authentication decision on its own** — a lookup-hash match only narrows to a candidate row, which is still bcrypt-verified against `recoveryCodeHash` before recovery succeeds (`findFellowshipTokenByRecoveryCode` rewritten: indexed lookup → bcrypt-verify → only on a miss, fall back to the old O(n) scan). **Why a new column instead of a backfill:** bcrypt is one-way — there is no way to recompute a lookup hash for a token created before this shipped, because the plaintext code was never stored anywhere to recompute it from. Pre-migration tokens keep `recoveryCodeLookupHash = NULL` forever and permanently use the O(n) fallback path — but that fallback set is fixed at whatever existed at cutover and only shrinks (as old tokens are recovered or retired), never grows again, since every token created from this deploy onward gets the column populated at creation. 5 new tests for the lookup-hash function (determinism, normalization parity with the real bcrypt path, uniqueness, correct digest length/format, distinctness from both the bcrypt hash and the plaintext code) — the DB-touching lookup function itself follows this codebase's established precedent of not being directly unit-tested, verified via `tsc --noEmit` instead. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `fellowship-token.test.ts` **14/14**, full `vitest.unit.config.ts` gate **602/602** (against a checkout that also includes the separately-packaged Fellowship merge-dedup fix; against a checkout without it, the gate is 597/597 plus these 5 new tests). `.env.example` updated to document `JWT_SECRET`'s new dual purpose and that rotating it is safe (affected tokens just fall back to the O(n) path, nothing breaks). **Prod: migration `db:apply-0073` required post-deploy** — adds the column and index only; existing tokens need no further action, new tokens use the fast path automatically from the next deploy. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

| 2026-07-19 | Claude | **Gap-analysis item #15 — actually closed now, not just "partly" (code-only, no migration).** Re-examining the two-clock downgrade mechanism (built 2026-07-17 for item #12(a)) to resolve #15's last open thread — the success-track window durations, previously only a documented inference — surfaced a real bug rather than just a documentation gap: `CANDIDATE_SUCCESS` (the success track's bottom rung, structurally equivalent to SIGNAL) had a `reviewWindowDaysFor()` entry but was **completely missing** from `DOWNGRADE_THRESHOLD_DAYS`, and the `ACTIVE → UNDER_REVIEW` transition branch only ever checked for `confidenceLevel === "SIGNAL"` by name. Net effect: a `CANDIDATE_SUCCESS` pattern could sit unconfirmed indefinitely and never be flagged stale by the automated fallback — an asymmetry between the two tracks' bottom rungs, not a deliberate design choice. Fixed: added `CANDIDATE_SUCCESS: 6 * 30` to `DOWNGRADE_THRESHOLD_DAYS`, and extended the SIGNAL-only branch to also match `CANDIDATE_SUCCESS` (same `UNDER_REVIEW` treatment, same 182-day re-review window). Updated the one existing test that had pinned the old (buggy) behavior — it previously asserted `downgradeThresholdDaysFor("CANDIDATE_SUCCESS")` was `undefined`; now asserts it equals `6 * 30`, with a new test asserting the *genuinely* rule-less case (`RETIRED`) instead. Doc comments in `fpkb-pattern-detector.ts` updated to record the fix and to state plainly which numbers (the day-counts themselves) remain a documented inference rather than fact, versus which parts are now settled (the mechanism applies symmetrically to both tracks — that part is fixed, not just asserted). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `fpkb-pattern-detector.test.ts` **13/13 passing**, full `vitest.unit.config.ts` gate **597/597**. **Prod: code-only, no migration** — this only changes which patterns the existing daily `fpkb:detect-patterns`/downgrade cron job flags, nothing new to run. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

Worse: this file's OWN header comment, written back in gap-analysis #9, already correctly cited "18 months" — meaning the record had it right before it was wrongly "corrected." Flagged plainly rather than left standing. **Second finding:** `kb_review_schedule` (§7.2/§7.4's task-queue table) has existed in the schema since migration 0057 but was completely unreferenced by any code — nothing wrote to it, nothing read it, and the Knowledge Stewardship page had no "Pattern review queue" tab despite §7.4 requiring one. What shipped in #14 was a fully automated, silent downgrade with no human-review step at all. **Reconciliation (not a doc conflict after all, on closer reading):** Observation Architecture §7.3's `reviewDueAt` (6/12 months) and FPKB_SCHEMA's §7.2 thresholds (18/24/12/6 months) are complementary, not contradictory — two different clocks for two different purposes. `reviewDueAt` passing creates a *soft* human review task; only if that goes unaddressed for the much longer §7.2 window does the *automated* fallback downgrade fire. This gives Knowledge Stewardship a real chance to intervene before a still-valid, hard-won pattern (each one backed by real children's outcomes) gets silently demoted — directly serving the mission, not just a technical nicety. **One apparent oddity examined and NOT flagged as a constitutional amendment:** §7.2 has ESTABLISHED downgrading faster (18mo) than CONFIRMED (24mo) — backwards from the naive expectation. Considered proposing a fix, then found a coherent rationale that survives scrutiny (an ESTABLISHED pattern with 100+ observations going silent for 18 months is a stronger anomaly signal than the same silence on a sparser CONFIRMED pattern) — implemented exactly as written, not "corrected." **Shipped:** `runConfidenceDowngrade` rewritten for the two-clock design (`server/lib/fpkb-pattern-detector.ts`); `fpkb.listPatternReviewQueue` + `fpkb.completePatternReview` tRPC procedures; a new "Review queue" tab on the Knowledge Stewardship page (with a live count badge); the whole pipeline (pattern detection + review-task creation + downgrade fallback) now runs on a **daily 01:00 Nairobi-time cron job** (§7.2's literal instruction — this is the one scheduled job in `server/scheduler.ts` given an explicit `timezone: "Africa/Nairobi"` option, since the doc specifies a real-world time, unlike this file's other EAT-labeled-but-not-actually-timezone-aware jobs, flagged as a separate, smaller follow-up worth revisiting, not fixed here). The manual CLI (`pnpm run fpkb:detect-patterns`) still works for on-demand runs/dry-run inspection. **A real bug caught by the tests written for this, worth noting:** SIGNAL's automated-downgrade threshold (180 days, from 6×30) came out slightly *shorter* than its review-task window (182 days) — investigated and left as-is deliberately (§7.2 sets both of SIGNAL's clocks to the same 6 months; moving to Under Review is a soft, reversible flag, not a real confidence loss, so the two-clock buffer matters less there) — but this is exactly the kind of off-by-2-days edge case that's easy to miss without a test asserting the ordering explicitly. 4 new tests (exact threshold values pinned so a future edit can't quietly revert them; the two-clock ordering invariant for the three consequential tiers). `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, full `server/` suite **1822/1873** (7 pre-existing unrelated failures, unchanged). **Prod: code-only, no migration** — `kb_review_schedule` already existed from migration 0057; this just starts using it. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

| 2026-07-17 | Claude | **Closed a flagged follow-up: `providerProfiles` didn't cache locality (gap-analysis #11/#10 geo work), and answered whether Care Signal needs a Safe-Truth-style matching job (it doesn't — see below):** CEO asked whether Care Signal needed a facility fuzzy-matching job like Safe-Truth's Phase C, since facility data doesn't always "auto-populate." Investigated before building anything: **the two symptoms are different problems.** Safe-Truth needs matching because caregivers type a facility name as free text with no structured record at all — there's a real unmatched string to resolve. Care Signal providers always pick from a real search result or explicitly create a "community facility," so every Care Signal event already has a real `facilityId` — there's nothing to fuzzy-match. The actual gap was narrower and simpler: `providerProfiles` (the cached row used to prefill the Care Signal form on a *return* visit, before any fresh search happens) had a `facilityRegion` (county) column but no locality column at all — so a provider's second-and-later sessions wouldn't show their locality even though a fresh search would. **Also corrected a claim from the original #11 flag:** `setMyFacility`'s return value already carried `adminLevel2` correctly the whole time (via `getFacilityById`, fixed in the original geo work) — it just wasn't consumed anywhere, so it wasn't actually broken, contrary to what was originally flagged. Fixed the real gap: added `providerProfiles.facilityAdminLevel2` (migration 0068), `syncProviderProfileFacility` now sets it on every named submission, and `FacilityPicker.tsx`'s prefill effect reads it. **No backfill job needed** — existing providers' cached profiles self-heal the next time they submit a named Care Signal event, since the sync function runs on every such submission already. **On the separate question this surfaces, worth flagging honestly:** if a facility's *own* record in the `facilities`/`careFacilities` table is simply missing county/locality data (not a code bug — genuinely absent data), no matching job can invent it; that's the scope of the already-tracked, separate Facility Phase 4 backfill effort, not something to build fresh here. `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, `vitest.unit.config.ts` gate **593/593**, full `server/` suite **1818/1869** (7 pre-existing unrelated failures, unchanged). **Prod: migration `db:apply-0068` required post-deploy; otherwise self-healing, no other manual step.** | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

| 2026-07-17 | Claude | **Gap-analysis #11 Phase C — facility fuzzy-matching + Care Signal event-code linkage (Event Models §2), all three phases of #11 now code-complete:** Built the two jobs the CEO explicitly asked not to defer indefinitely. **Facility matching:** `server/lib/facility-name-similarity.ts` — Sørensen–Dice coefficient over character bigrams (handles word-order differences, e.g. "Mathari Consolata Hospital" vs "Consolata Hospital Mathari"; chosen over Levenshtein for simplicity, no dependency needed). `server/lib/safe-truth-facility-matcher.ts` resolves `safeTruthSubmissions.facilityIdMatched` and `safeTruthFacilityVisits.visitFacilityIdMatched`, candidates pre-filtered to the same country + admin_level_1 (the CEO-approved design decision that made a simple string-similarity match tractable at all — not attempting a global fuzzy search). **Known dependency, flagged not hidden:** match quality is bounded by how complete the `facilities` table is (Facility Phase 4's ongoing, separate backfill effort) — a facility not in that table yet simply can't be matched; submissions stay unmatched and are retried safely on every future run. **Event-code linkage:** resolves a caregiver's entered code (`eventCodeEntered`) against a real `careSignalEvents.eventId` into a new `eventCodeResolvedCareSignalEventId` column — also safe to re-run, so a code entered before the provider's own Care Signal submission exists will resolve automatically once it does. | 2026-07-15 | Claude | **Gap-analysis #10 — Fellowship pseudonymous token model (Observation Architecture §5.5), CEO-approved design, shipped in full:** Built the three-way Care Signal submission model (`named` / `pseudonymous` / `anonymous`) to replace the old binary anonymous checkbox. **Discovery made while starting this item (flagged to CEO before proceeding, not silently patched):** the existing "Submit anonymously" checkbox never actually removed `userId` from provider Care Signal submissions — it only hid identity from facility-facing views (PSOT §20.3 rule 2/4), while the UI copy claimed "No identity stored" and the sync logic gave Fellowship credit to *every* provider submission including "anonymous" ones. Layer 2 offered per-submission regardless of login state (Care Signal itself still requires a real account — see fellowshipTokens' schema doc comment for the resulting, disclosed residual risk: server-side request logs could in principle correlate a session to a token at creation time, which no in-app token scheme can fully prevent); "revoke" hides the Fellow title display only, does not unlink or re-anonymize already-earned credit (mirrors the anonymize-not-delete precedent from #13); forward-looking only, no retroactive backfill of historical anonymous-but-linked rows (flagged as a possible future cleanup, not done here). **Shipped:** migration 0064 (`fellowshipTokens` table; `care-signal-events.ts`'s submission mutation rewired so identity storage and the rate-limit/dedup guard both key off `submissionMode` (documented consequence: true anonymous submissions have no persistent identity to rate-limit against — an inherent trade-off of Layer 1 meaning what it says); recovery-code lookup is O(n) in total tokens created (fine at pilot scale, flagged for a future index if it grows); no merge/dedup logic if a provider contributes via both a named account and a separately linked token. 22 new tests (token generation/hashing, shared pillar-math parity between identity paths, payload-builder mode wiring) — `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean, full `server/` suite **1789/1840 passing** (7 pre-existing, unrelated failures confirmed identical on a clean `main` checkout — not a regression), full `vitest.unit.config.ts` gate **538/538 passing**. **Prod:** migration `db:apply-0064` required post-deploy; New `runConfidenceDowngrade()` in `server/lib/fpkb-pattern-detector.ts`, called automatically as a second pass inside `pnpm run fpkb:detect-patterns` (same dry-run-by-default CLI, no new script needed). Implements the rule exactly as quoted from the doc (see code comment — corrects an earlier misstatement of specific "18/24/12/6 month" thresholds that turned out not to actually exist in the doc; caught by re-reading the source before implementing, not after): CONFIRMED/ESTABLISHED not reconfirmed within 12 months → downgrade one level; SIGNAL not reconfirmed within 6 months → `knowledge_status = UNDER_REVIEW`; UNDER_REVIEW not reconfirmed within a further 6 months → RETIRED (observation history preserved, just excluded from `listPatterns`' default ACTIVE filter). "Reconfirmed" reuses #9's existing mechanism — `recomputePatternConfidence` already extends `reviewDueAt` every time a pattern receives a new observation, so no separate reconfirmation tracking was needed. **Prerequisite bug in my own earlier code, fixed:** gap #3's `createPattern` never set an initial `reviewDueAt` on manually-curated patterns, so they would have silently never been caught by this downgrade job (`NULL < now()` never matches in SQL) — fixed by setting a 6-month initial window on creation, matching their starting SIGNAL/CANDIDATE_SUCCESS confidence level. **Two genuine doc ambiguities surfaced and flagged (queue item #15), not silently resolved by guessing:** (1) CANDIDATE's own review window is never explicitly stated — treated as 12 months by analogy to CONFIRMED/ESTABLISHED, matching the window #9 already assigned it; (2) §7.3's text only names failure-track levels — whether the same mechanism applies to the success track at all isn't stated; applied by structural analogy (one rung down per level) since Rule 8 ("every pattern has a mandatory review date") reads as track-agnostic in principle. 2 new unit tests for the downgrade path mappings (pure logic, no DB). New `server/lib/care-signal-deidentify.ts`: pattern-based redaction (known facility names cross-referenced against `careFacilities`/`facilities`, date patterns, phone numbers, emails, explicitly-labeled ID/passport numbers — deliberately does NOT redact bare numbers to avoid destroying clinical data like doses/vitals). **Documented limitation, not silently overclaimed:** this reliably catches structured identifiers but does NOT reliably catch free-text mentions of a person's name — flagged in code comments and in `DSAR_PROCEDURE.md`'s "other subjects' rights" section as needing manual review for that specific case; a stronger LLM-based redaction pass is a legitimate future option but a separate new-dependency/cost decision, not bundled into this one. New `server/lib/care-signal-anonymize.ts`: shared `anonymizeCareSignalEventRows`/`anonymizeCareSignalEventsForUser`, used by both `dsar-deletion.ts` and `retention-cleanup.ts` (previously each independently called `db.delete(careSignalEvents)`) so the two paths can't drift from each other. The anonymising UPDATE runs inside a `db.transaction()` with `@RAW_NARRATIVE_LEGAL_OVERRIDE_REASON` set on that transaction's pinned connection — this is precisely the legal-exception path the gap #8 trigger (migration 0061/0062) was built for, so every anonymisation is automatically audited to `care_signal_raw_narrative_audit` by the trigger itself, not by application code that could be bypassed. 9 new unit tests for the redaction logic (`care-signal-deidentify.test.ts`) — all pass; existing `dsar-deletion.test.ts`/`retention-cleanup.test.ts` still pass unchanged. **Constitutional docs updated to remove the ambiguity, not just the code:** `OBSERVATION_ARCHITECTURE_V1_1.md` Layer 0 row reconciled ("preserved permanently" now explicitly means the derived knowledge, not necessarily every raw sentence past its retention window) and its §10.2 status table's raw-narrative-immutability row marked DONE (with an honest flag that the rest of that status table is now stale and needs its own refresh pass — not silently left implying the whole table is current); `DATA_RETENTION_SCHEDULE.md` §4.1 and its master table's Care Signal row updated from "Batch purge job (TBD)" to the anonymise decision; `DSAR_PROCEDURE.md`'s §6 checklist line ("delete rows OR anonymise `userId` per counsel") resolved definitively, with the third-party-redaction limitation flagged inline. **Important — this is a CEO decision, not a substitute for legal sign-off:** both legal docs are still explicitly marked "counsel review" status: this implements the CEO's preferred direction so there's a concrete, working system to review rather than an open question, but if counsel later requires full hard-deletion instead, `care-signal-anonymize.ts` is the one place to change. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean, 12 relevant tests passing. **Prod:** code-only, no migration — the anonymisation path reuses the existing gap #8 trigger/audit infrastructure as-is. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |
| 2026-07-12 | Claude | **Correction to migration 0061 — SIGNAL MESSAGE_TEXT too long (gap #8 follow-up, REAL MIGRATION):** Running `db:apply-0061` in production, then `verify:raw-narrative-immutable`, surfaced a real bug: the trigger's `SIGNAL SQLSTATE '45000' SET MESSAGE_TEXT = '...'` string was 174 characters; MySQL hard-caps `MESSAGE_TEXT` at 128. **The immutability constraint itself worked correctly** — the plain UPDATE was genuinely blocked — but the SIGNAL statement then failed with MySQL's own "Data too long for condition item 'MESSAGE_TEXT'" instead of the intended readable error. This is exactly why the verify script exercises the trigger against a real (rolled-back) transaction instead of only checking it exists — a "looks right" migration script had a real defect that only running it caught. New corrective migration `scripts/apply-0062-fix-raw-narrative-trigger-message.mjs`: drops and recreates the same trigger with a 98-character message, logic otherwise unchanged. Fixed `apply-0061-raw-narrative-immutable.mjs`'s source too, so a fresh install (new environment, not this production DB) creates the correct trigger the first time. **Prod:** run `pnpm run db:apply-0062`, then re-run `pnpm run verify:raw-narrative-immutable` to confirm both checks now pass. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |
| 2026-07-12 | Claude | **Raw narrative immutability constraint (gap-analysis #8 — REAL MIGRATION, requires `pnpm run db:apply-0061` post-merge):** Observation Architecture Principle 2 requires `raw_narrative` (the only such column — `careSignalEvents.raw_narrative`) be immutable after submission except for a legal requirement with explicit audit trail. This was a discipline convention only, enforced nowhere. New migration `scripts/apply-0061-raw-narrative-immutable.mjs` (idempotent): creates `care_signal_raw_narrative_audit` (append-only) and a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger on `careSignalEvents` that SIGNALs a SQL error blocking any change to `raw_narrative` unless a session variable `@RAW_NARRATIVE_LEGAL_OVERRIDE_REASON` is set to a non-empty string — deliberately not exposed through the application, only reachable via direct DB access, matching the "legal requirement" carve-out. When the override is used, the trigger itself writes the audit row, so the trail can't be skipped even via raw SQL as long as the override path (not disabling the trigger) is used. New `scripts/verify-0061-raw-narrative-immutable.mjs` actually exercises the trigger inside a rolled-back transaction — confirms a plain UPDATE is rejected AND the override path succeeds and is audited — rather than just checking the trigger exists. Added `careSignalRawNarrativeAudit` Drizzle binding and a `getRawNarrativeAuditLog` admin transparency query (no dedicated UI — this table should almost always be empty; its existence matters more than a display surface). **Important discovery, NOT fixed in this PR, added as queue item #13:** `server/lib/dsar-deletion.ts` and `server/lib/retention-cleanup.ts` both hard-DELETE `careSignalEvents` rows entirely (destroying `raw_narrative`), which contradicts Observation Architecture's own Layer 0 table ("On account deletion: userId set null, narrative retained... Preserved permanently"). This is a genuine privacy-vs-research-retention tension, not a bug I should silently resolve — it needs your decision (possibly with legal input given GDPR/Kenya DPA right-to-erasure obligations), so I flagged it rather than changing deletion/compliance code unilaterally. **Verification honesty note:** I validated the migration's SQL by careful reading (standard MySQL trigger syntax; confirmed `mysql2` sends the whole `CREATE TRIGGER` as one protocol-level statement so no `DELIMITER` handling is needed) and `node --check` on both scripts for JS syntax, but could not execute it against a real MySQL instance from this sandbox — unlike the TypeScript changes elsewhere, `tsc` can't validate trigger SQL. Recommend running `db:apply-0061` then immediately `verify:raw-narrative-immutable` (safe — everything it does is rolled back) rather than assuming success from a clean-looking script. Verified otherwise: `tsc --noEmit` clean, PWA tsconfig clean. **Prod:** run `pnpm run db:apply-0061`, then `pnpm run verify:raw-narrative-immutable`. | PR _(packaged as zip, not yet opened — see chat)_ |

| 14 | Automated confidence downgrade on staleness (Observation Architecture §7.3 concept drift management). **DONE** — see dated entry above, PR not yet opened. **Correction:** the original flagging of this item (above) stated specific "18/24/12/6 month" thresholds that, on re-reading the doc before implementing, don't actually appear anywhere in it — that was my own misremembering, caught before building against it, not after. The real, quoted rule is simpler: 12 months for CONFIRMED/ESTABLISHED (downgrade one level), 6 months for SIGNAL (→ UNDER_REVIEW), a further 6 months for UNDER_REVIEW (→ RETIRED). Two genuine doc ambiguities surfaced and flagged rather than silently resolved — see new item #15. |
| 15 | **Discovered 2026-07-12 while building #14; re-examined 2026-07-15 and 2026-07-19 — see dated entries below.** Of the two doc ambiguities originally flagged: (1) CANDIDATE's review window — **resolved, was a bug, now fixed.** §7.3's prose alone never states it, but §6.6's Pattern Record field table does: "6 months for Signal and Candidate." The shipped code had guessed 12 months (wrong); corrected in `fpkb-pattern-detector.ts` via a shared `reviewWindowDaysFor()` helper, with regression tests. (2) Whether the downgrade mechanism applies to the success track — **mechanism confirmed yes** (§6.6 explicitly frames `review_schedule`/`knowledge_status` as "Both Tracks" fields); applying that consistently on 2026-07-19 found `CANDIDATE_SUCCESS` (the success track's own bottom rung) was missing from the automated-downgrade threshold table entirely, meaning it could never be flagged stale — a real bug, not just a documentation gap. Fixed. | **DONE** — both the CANDIDATE bug (2026-07-15) and the CANDIDATE_SUCCESS bug (2026-07-19) are fixed and tested. What remains a documented inference rather than fact: the exact success-track day-counts themselves (182/182/365/365 review windows; 180/360/720/540 downgrade thresholds) — nothing in either constitutional doc states them, only the rank-analogy to the equivalent failure-track tier. Low priority, flagged in code, not blocking. |

**Working convention for this queue:** each item ships as its own small PR (per AGENTS.md §9 "small, reviewable changes only"), packaged as a zip since CEO applies changes locally via PowerShell `Expand-Archive` (no direct sandbox push access); each PR gets a dated `WORK_STATUS.md` entry and, where a migration is needed, exact `pnpm run db:apply-NNNN` instructions for the CEO to run against production. Items 10 and 11 need explicit CEO sign-off on approach before code is written, per AGENTS.md §9 clinical/architecture approval rules.

| 2026-07-21 | Claude (other account) | **Fix: `scripts/pre-merge-check.ps1` failed to parse on Windows PowerShell 5.1 — "Missing closing '}' in statement block," despite balanced braces.** Root cause: the script used Unicode em dashes; PowerShell 5.1 (the default `powershell.exe`) can misread a UTF-8 `.ps1` file via the system codepage, corrupting multi-byte characters and breaking token boundaries downstream in a way that surfaces as a brace-matching error. Fix: rewritten as pure ASCII, no em dashes or smart quotes. Lesson: PowerShell scripts in this repo should stick to plain ASCII, unlike the `.md`/`.ts` convention of using typographic characters freely. | PR _(applied directly, no PR)_ |
